- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Isabella, leader of Spain (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Spain
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Conquistador, Replacing Explorer (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Inquisition, Replacing Inquisitor (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Armada, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Bullring, Replacing Zoo (Requires technology: Scientific Theory)
        - Unique Improvement: Hacienda (Cannot be built adjacent to another Hacienda. Gains additional yields for each adjacent Resource:
          +3 Food from Bonus Resources
          +3 Production from Strategic Resources
          +3 Gold from Luxury Resources); Requires technology: Steel
        - Civilization Ability: +10 Gold and +4 Faith from gaining tiles, scaling with Era. Newly founded Cities adopt the Majority Religion of your Capital. May purchase Naval Units with Faith.
      - Leader: Isabella
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Policies
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Majesty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Specialists in Capital consume half the normal amount of Food.
        - * Palace Gardens built in Capital (+5 Food, +25% Great Person Rate, -2 Unhappiness from Urbanization, 1 Great Work of Writing slot, 1 Writer Slot).
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: NUCLEAR WARRIOR PERSONA - VICTORY IMMINENT (5 TURNS): World Congress voting begins next turn. 65 delegates secured vs 42 threshold. Maximum aggression maintained to eliminate Japan/Hunnic threats before diplomatic victory. Shoshone vassal loyalty absolute (8,880 diplomat network, +70/turn). Will use nuclear weapons if necessary against Huns for Duckwater atomic threat. Total war posture against all ideological opponents (Freedom/Order) until World Congress confirms victory. Zero diplomatic compromise, zero strategic patience required.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Vancouver
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: Vancouver alliance is extremely secure at 188 influence with shared Catholicism. Natural Wonder quest completed (Barringer Crater discovered), further cementing the relationship. Reduced from previous high urgency as they're now permanently allied. Maintaining modest positive modifiers to preserve this valuable gateway city-state without over-investing.
        - UpdatedTurn: 171
        
        ### Huari
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Peace has been achieved with Huari (their city converted to Catholicism this turn). The +60 modifiers are no longer needed and can be cancelled. We can now focus fully on the Shoshone campaign without worrying about a two-front war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 178
        
        ### Spain
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Cancelling +100 Shoshone peace modifiers. Despite 100% war weariness, they refuse peace. Cannot waste diplomatic effort - need to focus 100% on Egypt who is 5 turns from cultural victory. Will accept peace if offered but no longer pursuing it.
        - UpdatedTurn: 310
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: NETHERLANDS PEACE NORMALIZATION: Peace established (Turn 402). Removed +80 modifiers - no longer need to pressure them as they're at peace and Hunnic vassal. Neutral stance (0/0) maintains status quo without antagonizing. They remain weak (4 cities, 1802 military strength) and non-threat. Focus diplomatic energy on Egypt/Celts instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 402
        
        ### Lhasa
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: LHASA ALLIANCE SECURED: Lhasa is secure ally with 875 influence, majority Catholic, and trade route established. No emergency modifiers needed. Focus diplomatic energy on war efforts.
        - UpdatedTurn: 440
        
        ### The Celts
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: CELTIC FINAL ANNIHILATION PHASE CONTINUES: Edinburgh reduced to 27 population after nuclear strike, city at minimal health. Nantes under XCOM siege. Total elimination required before pivoting to Egyptian theater. Celtic 19 delegates currently contribution to WC voting - their elimination removes these from opposition. Maximum pressure maintains rapid conquest tempo.
        - UpdatedTurn: 463
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: JAPAN NEAR ELIMINATION - KYOTO CAPTURED: Successfully captured Kyoto with XCOM squad paradrop assault! Japan reduced to Osaka (new capital) and irradiated Kagoshima (pop 12). Complete annihilation continues to eliminate their 4 opposition votes before World Congress. 100% war weariness but they refuse peace. Total war until capitulation or elimination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 488
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: EGYPT CULTURAL VICTORY DETERRENCE: 30 turns from cultural victory, Order ideology opponent with Thebes at 0 health (vulnerable). Maximum hostility prevents diplomatic interference in final World Congress phase. Spies active for intelligence. Will nuke Thebes if cultural victory accelerates. Cannot allow competitor to surge while securing diplomatic win.
        - UpdatedTurn: 488
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 20
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: ROME DIPLOMATIC NEUTRALIZATION: Peace treaty active (9 turns remaining). Rome has 6 votes and is Hunnic vassal - not worth antagonizing. +20 modifiers encourage stability and prevent them from lobbying against my World Congress bid. They're weak (Medieval era, 305 strength) and pose zero military threat. Better to keep them docile than risk them influencing delegates against me.
        - UpdatedTurn: 494
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: SHOSHONE FINAL LOYALTY LOCK - 6 TURNS TO VICTORY: Absolute loyalty maintained despite atomic proliferation concerns. Diplomat network now 9,580 (+70/turn) with 30 delegates essential for Turn 503 World Congress victory threshold (42). Vassal state contentment high with shared Autocracy/Catholicism. No diplomatic rupture risk acceptable - maintaining +100 modifiers ensures unwavering allegiance through final vote. Victory imminent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 497
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: HUNNIC ATOMIC THREAT - FINAL DEFENSE POSTURE: Three atomic bombs remain stationed at Duckwater (events confirm no launch this turn). Freedom ideology opponent with 21 delegates continues opposing World Congress interests. Total war posture maintained to prevent nuclear strikes on coastal cities during critical final 6 turns. Maximum hostility deters diplomatic interference. No compromise until victory secured.
        - UpdatedTurn: 497
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: FINAL FOUR TURNS TO DIPLOMATIC VICTORY: World Congress voting begins in 5 turns (Turn 503) with 39/42 delegates secured. Shoshone vassal maintains absolute loyalty with 9,650 diplomat network providing critical 30 votes. Barcelona completing SS Stasis Chamber in 2 turns provides science victory backup. Huns atomic threat persists (3 bombs at Duckwater) requiring maintained defensive vigilance. Current NUCLEAR WARRIOR persona and aggressive military flavors optimal for deterring last-minute interference. No strategic adjustments needed - victory convergence assured. Maintain current UnitedNations grand strategy and flavor priorities through World Congress confirmation.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 4 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Zuunmod:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Almaty:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Brussels:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Spanish Sphere of Influence: Lhasa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Vancouver:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Vienna:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: La Venta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Cape Town:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Spain
          - Repeal: Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - Proposer: The Shoshone
          - Enact: Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - Proposer: The Huns

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Isabella, leader of Spain (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Spain
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Conquistador, Replacing Explorer (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Inquisition, Replacing Inquisitor (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Armada, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Bullring, Replacing Zoo (Requires technology: Scientific Theory)
        - Unique Improvement: Hacienda (Cannot be built adjacent to another Hacienda. Gains additional yields for each adjacent Resource:
          +3 Food from Bonus Resources
          +3 Production from Strategic Resources
          +3 Gold from Luxury Resources); Requires technology: Steel
        - Civilization Ability: +10 Gold and +4 Faith from gaining tiles, scaling with Era. Newly founded Cities adopt the Majority Religion of your Capital. May purchase Naval Units with Faith.
      - Leader: Isabella
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Policies
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Majesty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Specialists in Capital consume half the normal amount of Food.
        - * Palace Gardens built in Capital (+5 Food, +25% Great Person Rate, -2 Unhappiness from Urbanization, 1 Great Work of Writing slot, 1 Writer Slot).
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - rationale: Barcelona is one turn away from completing the SS Stasis Chamber, and with five spaceship parts already finished (83% progress), Science Victory is imminent—likely only one component remains. Switching the GrandStrategy from UnitedNations to Spaceship guarantees that any final spaceship part will be treated as the top priority in all cities once their current queues finish. I am significantly reducing Offense, Mobilization, Nuke, and Naval flavors because our military is already dominant (over 49k strength) and we are hemorrhaging more than 1,200 Gold per turn; dialing back unnecessary unit production will stem the treasury bleed while keeping existing forces in the field. Production, Gold, and Science are kept high to rush the final component, while Defense and CityDefense remain adequate to manage the ongoing war with The Huns until launch.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  We are at turn 499 in a late-game scenario. Let me analyze the current state carefully.
            
            **Victory Progress:**
            - **Domination Victory**: The Huns control 5 capitals (62%), Spain controls 3 (37%). The Huns are the contender here.
            - **Science Victory**: Spain has 83% progress, 5 parts completed, Apollo completed. Rome and The Huns have Apollo but 0 parts. Spain is the clear contender.
            - **Cultural Victory**: Spain is influential over The Shoshone, popular over The Huns (rising, 30 turns to influential), and popular over Rome (rising slowly). The Huns are dominant over Rome. Shoshone is influential over Rome. Spain needs to influence Rome and The Huns. Currently at 1/3 influential.
            - **Diplomatic Victory**: World Congress voting in 4 turns. Spain has 39 delegates (92% victory percentage), needs 42. The Shoshone has 30 (71%), The Huns 21 (50%). Spain proposed International Games. Spain is the contender.
            
            **Current Situation:**
            - Spain is at war with The Huns. War weariness is 53% for Spain, 13% for Huns. War score is 6% (slightly favorable).
            - Spain has a massive military strength of 49,130 vs Huns' 40,134. Spain has advanced units (Stealth Bombers, Jet Fighters, Giant Death Robots, Modern Armor, XCOM).
            - Spain is in the Information Era with 82 technologies. Researching Future Tech (6 turns).
            - The Huns are also in Information Era with 74 techs.
            - Spain has 32 cities, 865 population. Very strong economy but losing 1240 gold per turn (probably due to military and building maintenance).
            - Happiness is at 61%, which is okay but not great.
            - Spain has a vassal: The Shoshone (friendly relationship, both Autocracy).
            - Spain is building spaceship parts in some cities (SS Stasis Chamber in Barcelona, production turns left: 1).
            - Spain has built the United Nations.
            
            **Strategic Considerations:**
            1. **Science Victory**: This seems very close. Spain has 5/6 parts completed (83%). Barcelona is finishing SS Stasis Chamber in 1 turn. Need to check what parts remain. Usually there are 6 parts: SS Cockpit, SS Stasis Chamber, SS Engine, SS Life Support, SS Habitation, and maybe another? In VP it might vary, but 83% with 5 parts means probably 1 part left. But Barcelona is about to finish one. Wait - if parts percentage is 83% and 5 parts completed, there might be 6 parts total, so 1 remaining. Or more parts in VP. Either way, this is almost done. Madrid appears to have built Strategic Defense System, not a spaceship part. Wait, looking at cities:
               - Barcelona: SS Stasis Chamber (1 turn left)
               - No other obvious spaceship part production visible in the city list... Actually looking at the building lists, I see "Spaceship Factory" in many cities but the current productions are mostly wealth/arts/military/buildings.
               - Madrid just finished Strategic Defense System.
               - We need to look at what the remaining part is. With 83% and 5 parts done, likely 1 more part needed.
            
            2. **Diplomatic Victory**: 39 delegates, need 42. Voting in 4 turns. If we can secure 3 more delegates, we win. We have many city-state allies but The Shoshone also has many. We proposed International Games which might give us a boost. The Shoshone is our vassal and has 30 delegates - can they vote for us? In VP, vassals typically vote for their master in some implementations, but I'm not entirely sure. We also have Rome at friendly+20. Active resolutions include many Shoshone spheres of influence.
            
            3. **Domination/Cultural**: Not the immediate path.
            
            **Immediate Tactical Situation:**
            - The war with The Huns is ongoing. We're doing decently (6% war score) but we have high war weariness (53%).
            - We have a massive advanced military and are pushing into Hunnic territory around Kyoto/Osaka/Hiroshima.
            - We're also fighting around Almaty (city-state).
            - Several cities are building infinite projects (wealth, arts, farming) - this is late game when buildings are done.
            - We have -1240 GPT but 30,236 gold in treasury. We can sustain this for ~24 turns. But we should consider if we need this massive military anymore.
            
            **Policy and Tech:**
            - Next policy in 15 turns.
            - Next tech: Future Tech (6 turns). After that, no more useful techs for science victory.
            - We haven't set a next research or policy.
            
            **Flavors/Strategy:**
            - Current Grand Strategy: UnitedNations
            - Current flavors are heavily military-focused (Offense 90, Defense 60, Mobilization 80, Ranged 100, Mobile 100, Nuke 100, etc.) with high Diplomacy (100) and Production (100).
            - This made sense for the war and diplomatic push.
            
            **Decision Points:**
            1. **Grand Strategy**: Should we switch to Spaceship to prioritize the final spaceship part? Or stay UnitedNations for diplomatic win? We can pursue both - we have 4 turns until UN vote and maybe 6+ turns until final spaceship part.
            2. **Military**: We have a huge military and are losing money. If we're close to science or diplo victory, we might not need such extreme military focus. But we are at war with the Huns.
            3. **War/Peace**: We could try to make peace with The Huns to reduce war weariness and expenses, but they have 5 capitals and we might want them for domination. Actually, we only have 3 capitals. But domination seems far since Huns have 5 and Rome is allied with them.
            4. **Gold**: We're bleeding gold. The military is expensive. If we reduce military flavors, the AI might disband or not replace units, saving money.
            5. **Next Research**: Future Tech is almost done. After Future Tech, there's nothing useful. Actually we should keep researching Future Tech for score, but it doesn't provide new benefits.
            6. **Next Policy**: We could pick something useful. We're in Autocracy with many tenets. Looking at policy options, we have: Lebensraum, Ceremony, Colonialism, Expertise, Regimental System, Rights of Man, Shadow Networks, Nobility, Majesty, Trade Confederacy, Division of Labor, Tribute, Enlightenment, Free Trade, Artistry.
               - Many of these are from branches we haven't fully committed to.
               - Artistry is a new branch. Since we have many great works and are generating tourism, Artistry could help cultural victory, but that's not our immediate path.
               - Enlightenment gives +1 Free Tech, which is useless since we've finished all techs.
               - Rights of Man gives -1 unhappiness from needs, +2 production/gold from villages, +10% science during Golden Ages. We have 10 turns of Golden Age left.
               - Trade Confederacy gives +25% yields for international trade routes. With Cold War active, we can only trade with same ideology (Autocracy: Shoshone vassal) or allied city-states. We have many trade routes to Shoshone and city-states.
               - Shadow Networks gives 25% science from allied city-states. We have many city-state allies.
               - Division of Labor gives +3% production/gold from industrial buildings.
               - Expertise gives +15% production toward buildings and +10 culture per building.
               
               None of these are game-changing. We don't have a next policy for 15 turns anyway.
            
            **Key Immediate Issue:**
            The game is about to end via either Science or Diplomacy. We are very close to both. We need to make sure we don't lose the war or economy before then.
            
            Looking at the victory conditions:
            - Diplomatic: 39/42 delegates, voting in 4 turns. We need 3 more. Can we get them?
              - We might be able to get more city-state allies or bask in international games completion.
              - The Shoshone is our vassal with 30 delegates. They might vote for us. But in Civ5 VP, World Congress voting is complex. Actually, the UI says "VictoryPercentage: 92" for Spain and "Votes: 39", need 42.
              - If International Games completes, we get +2 tourism and maybe other benefits, but not delegates.
              - We could try to get more city-state allies before the vote in 4 turns, but that's hard.
              - Wait, the active resolution "Cold War" prevents trade with non-allied, non-same-ideology civs, but doesn't affect delegates directly.
              - Actually, if we win the International Games (we proposed it), we might get tourism and a free great person.
              - World Ideology: Autocracy gives us extra delegates if others follow it. Rome and Huns follow... Huns have Freedom, Rome has ? Rome's ideology isn't explicitly stated, but The Huns chose Freedom. We have Autocracy. The Shoshone has Autocracy.
              - The resolution for World Ideology: Autocracy is active but EnactedOn: -1, meaning proposed but not enacted? Wait, looking at ActiveResolutions, it's listed there with EnactedOn: -1. That might mean it's proposed, not enacted. Actually, if enacted, it should have a positive turn number. So it's on the docket? No, looking at the structure, ActiveResolutions are currently active laws. EnactedOn: -1 might mean it's been proposed and pending. But there's a vote in 4 turns.
              
            - Science: 83%. Let's assume we have 1 part left. If Barcelona finishes SS Stasis Chamber next turn, we'll be at 5 parts + 1 completed = 6 parts? Or if there are more than 6 parts in VP... Actually in VP science victory requires more parts I think. Let me think. The report says "PartsPercentage: 83, PartsCompleted: 5". If 5 parts = 83%, then total parts = ~6. So 1 part left. But we need to check what part is missing and make sure a city builds it. Looking at cities, I don't see another spaceship part in production. Madrid finished Strategic Defense System. Barcelona finishes SS Stasis Chamber. We should ensure the final part gets built. But since we don't see it in production, maybe the AI already queued it somewhere or we need to set flavors to prioritize it.
            
            Wait, the GrandStrategy is UnitedNations, but we have Spaceship flavor at 60. If we switch GrandStrategy to Spaceship, the AI will prioritize spaceship parts.
            
            **Critical Decision:**
            We are very close to Science Victory (1 part left?) and Diplomatic Victory (3 delegates short in 4 turns). Given the uncertainty of diplomatic (can we really get 3 more delegates in 4 turns?), Science Victory might be more reliable IF we make sure the last part is built.
            
            However, looking at the production in cities: many are doing infinite projects. No visible spaceship part production besides Barcelona's SS Stasis Chamber finishing in 1 turn. This suggests either:
            1. The AI will automatically start the next part after this one
            2. We need to guide it
            
            But wait - if Barcelona is building SS Stasis Chamber and it's the LAST part, then next turn we might launch! No, 83% with 5 parts suggests maybe there are 7 or 8 total parts in VP? Let me reconsider. Civ5 BNW base game has 6 parts. VP might add more or change requirements. But PartsCompleted: 5, PartsPercentage: 83. If total was 6, 5/6 = 83.3%. So yes, exactly one part left.
            
            But which cities usually build which parts? In base game:
            - SS Cockpit (Capital)
            - SS Stasis Chamber
            - SS Engine
            - SS Life Support
            - SS Habitation
            - SS Booster (x3? No, that's parts)
            
            Wait, in BNW there are 6 parts total. In VP, there might be different parts or more. Regardless, 83% strongly implies 1 part remaining.
            
            If Barcelona is about to finish a part, and there's 1 left, we need to make sure some city builds the last one. The GrandStrategy Spaceship would help ensure this.
            
            But actually, Madrid has a Spaceship Factory and just finished Strategic Defense System. If we set GrandStrategy to Spaceship, Madrid or another city might start the final part.
            
            **Alternative: Diplomatic Victory**
            We need 3 more delegates. Current delegates: 39. Need: 42.
            Delegates come from:
            - City-state allies (we have many: Lhasa ally, others friends; Shoshone has many allies too)
            - World Religion (if our religion is world religion)
            - World Ideology (if our ideology is world ideology)
